    {
    "Title": "Deep Adaptive Design: Amortizing Sequential Bayesian Experimental Design",
    "Main Contributions": "Deep Adaptive Design (DAD) introduces a method to amortize the cost of adaptive Bayesian optimal experimental design (BOED), enabling experiments to be run in real-time. Traditional sequential BOED approaches are computationally intensive at each step, making them impractical for real-world applications requiring quick decisions. DAD learns an amortized design network upfront that acts as a design policy, taking past data and outputting the next design in milliseconds. It introduces contrastive information bounds suitable for the sequential setting and proposes a customized network architecture that exploits key symmetries. DAD successfully amortizes experimental design, outperforming alternative strategies on several problems, and can learn non-myopic adaptive strategies.",
    "Methodology": "DAD learns a design network, πφ, which maps from previous design-observation pairs (history, ht) to the next design (ξt). This network is trained by simulating hypothetical experimental trajectories to make near-optimal design decisions automatically. The core objective is reformulated from an iterative problem into a single holistic objective, IT(π), representing the total expected information gained from the entire experiment, eliminating the need for direct posterior or marginal likelihood estimation during the experiment. To optimize this intractable objective, DAD derives contrastive lower bounds called sequential PCE (sPCE) and uses stochastic gradient ascent for end-to-end training. For gradient estimation, it uses the reparameterization trick for continuous, reparametrizable likelihoods and a score function (REINFORCE) gradient estimator for discrete observations or non-reparametrizable likelihoods. The network architecture exploits permutation invariance of the BOED problem, representing history ht with a fixed-dimensional pooled representation R(ht) = ∑t k=1 Eφ1 (ξk,yk) (using an encoder Eφ1), and then passing this to an emitter network Fφ2 to output the design πφ(ht) = Fφ2 (R(ht)).",
    "Experimental Setup": "The DAD method was implemented using PyTorch and Pyro, with code publicly available. It was compared against several baselines: random design, fixed (batch) design (optimized using SG-BOED), and a variational baseline (SG-BOED used iteratively to approximate the optimal myopic policy, requiring runtime computation). Problem-specific adaptive strategies like Kirby (2009), Frye et al. (2016), Badapted (Vincent & Rainforth, 2017), and SeqBED (Kleinegesse et al., 2020) were also used for comparison. Performance metrics included the total expected information gain (IT(π)) estimated via sPCE lower and sNMC upper bounds, and deployment time. DAD was evaluated on three applications: 1) Location finding in 2D (acoustic energy attenuation model) with 2 hidden sources and T=30 experiments. 2) Hyperbolic temporal discounting in psychology, involving T=20 binary questions to model human decision-making. 3) A death process model from epidemiology, selecting T=4 observation times sequentially. Training involved Adam optimizer, exponential learning rate annealing, and varying numbers of inner and outer samples for gradient estimation.",
    "Limitations": "The current form of DAD requires the likelihood model to be explicit, meaning the density p(yt|θ,ξt) must be evaluable. It also assumes that experiments are conditionally independent given θ, i.e., p(y1:T|θ,ξ1:T) = ∏T t=1 p(yt|θ,ξt), which may not hold for certain models such as time series. Additionally, DAD requires the designs, ξt, to be continuous to allow for gradient-based optimization.",
    "Future Research Directions": "Future work could focus on addressing the requirement for explicit likelihood models and extending DAD to scenarios where experiments are not conditionally independent given θ, such as with time series models. Further research could also explore extending the method to handle discrete design spaces. Additionally, investigating the links between policy-based experimental design in DAD and model-based reinforcement learning, despite the absence of observed rewards, presents an interesting avenue for exploration.",
    "Experiment Code": "import math\n\nimport torch\nfrom torch.distributions.utils import broadcast_all\n\nimport pyro\nfrom pyro import poutine\nfrom pyro.poutine.util import prune_subsample_sites\nfrom pyro.util import warn_if_nan\nfrom pyro.infer.util import torch_item\nfrom pyro.contrib.util import lexpand, rexpand\n\n\nclass MutualInformation(object):\n    def __init__(self, num_outer_samples, data_source=None):\n        self.data_source = data_source\n        self.num_outer_samples = num_outer_samples\n\n    def _vectorized(self, fn, *shape, name=\"vectorization_plate\"):\n        \"\"\"\n        Wraps a callable inside an outermost :class:`~pyro.plate` to parallelize\n        MI computation over `num_particles`, and to broadcast batch shapes of\n        sample site functions in accordance with the `~pyro.plate` contexts\n        within which they are embedded.\n        :param fn: arbitrary callable containing Pyro primitives.\n        :return: wrapped callable.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        def wrapped_fn(*args, **kwargs):\n            with pyro.plate_stack(name, shape):\n                return fn(*args, **kwargs)\n\n        return wrapped_fn\n\n    def get_primary_rollout(self, model, args, kwargs, graph_type=\"flat\", detach=False):\n        if self.data_source is None:\n            model = self._vectorized(\n                model, self.num_outer_samples, name=\"outer_vectorization\"\n            )\n        else:\n            data = next(self.data_source)\n            model = pyro.condition(\n                self._vectorized(\n                    model, self.num_outer_samples, name=\"outer_vectorization\"\n                ),\n                data=data,\n            )\n\n        trace = poutine.trace(model, graph_type=graph_type).get_trace(*args, **kwargs)\n        if detach:\n            trace.detach_()\n        trace = prune_subsample_sites(trace)\n        trace.compute_log_prob()\n        return trace\n\n\nclass PriorContrastiveEstimation(MutualInformation):\n    def __init__(self, num_outer_samples=100, num_inner_samples=10, data_source=None):\n        super().__init__(num_outer_samples, data_source=data_source)\n        self.num_inner_samples = num_inner_samples\n\n    def compute_observation_log_prob(self, trace):\n        \"\"\"\n        Computes the log probability of observations given latent variables and designs.\n        :param trace: a Pyro trace object\n        :return: the log prob tensor\n        \"\"\"\n        return sum(\n            node[\"log_prob\"]\n            for node in trace.nodes.values()\n            if node.get(\"subtype\") == \"observation_sample\"\n        )\n\n    def get_contrastive_rollout(\n        self,\n        trace,\n        model,\n        args,\n        kwargs,\n        existing_vectorization,\n        graph_type=\"flat\",\n        detach=False,\n    ):\n        sampled_observation_values = {\n            name: lexpand(node[\"value\"], self.num_inner_samples)\n            for name, node in trace.nodes.items()\n            if node.get(\"subtype\") in [\"observation_sample\", \"design_sample\"]\n        }\n        conditional_model = self._vectorized(\n            pyro.condition(model, data=sampled_observation_values),\n            self.num_inner_samples,\n            *existing_vectorization,\n            name=\"inner_vectorization\",\n        )\n        trace = poutine.trace(conditional_model, graph_type=graph_type).get_trace(\n            *args, **kwargs\n        )\n        if detach:\n            trace.detach_()\n        trace = prune_subsample_sites(trace)\n        trace.compute_log_prob()\n\n        return trace\n\n    def differentiable_loss(self, model, *args, **kwargs):\n        \"\"\"\n        Computes the surrogate loss that can be differentiated with autograd\n        to produce gradient estimates for the design\n        \"\"\"\n        primary_trace = self.get_primary_rollout(model, args, kwargs)\n        contrastive_trace = self.get_contrastive_rollout(\n            primary_trace, model, args, kwargs, [self.num_outer_samples]\n        )\n\n        obs_log_prob_primary = self.compute_observation_log_prob(primary_trace)\n        obs_log_prob_contrastive = self.compute_observation_log_prob(contrastive_trace)\n\n        obs_log_prob_combined = torch.cat(\n            [lexpand(obs_log_prob_primary, 1), obs_log_prob_contrastive]\n        ).logsumexp(0)\n\n        loss = (obs_log_prob_combined - obs_log_prob_primary).mean(0)\n\n        warn_if_nan(loss, \"loss\")\n        return loss\n\n    def loss(self, model, *args, **kwargs):\n        \"\"\"\n        :returns: returns an estimate of the mutual information\n        :rtype: float\n        Evaluates the MI lower bound using prior contrastive estimation; == -EIG proxy\n        \"\"\"\n        loss_to_constant = torch_item(self.differentiable_loss(model, *args, **kwargs))\n        return loss_to_constant - math.log(self.num_inner_samples + 1)\n\n\nclass PriorContrastiveEstimationDiscreteObsTotalEnum(PriorContrastiveEstimation):\n    def __init__(self, num_outer_samples, num_inner_samples, data_source=None):\n        super().__init__(\n            num_outer_samples=num_outer_samples,\n            num_inner_samples=num_inner_samples,\n            data_source=data_source,\n        )\n        self.T = None\n        self.design_size = None\n        self.y_support_size = None\n        self.y_possible_paths = None\n\n    def get_all_possible_paths(self, model, *args, **kwargs):\n        \"\"\"Expand histories\"\"\"\n        main_model_trace = pyro.poutine.trace(model).get_trace()\n        obs_samples = [\n            node\n            for node in main_model_trace.nodes.values()\n            if node.get(\"subtype\") == \"observation_sample\"\n        ]\n        obs_support = obs_samples[0][\"fn\"].enumerate_support()\n        self.y_support_size = obs_support.shape[0]  # e.g for hyperbolic temp = 2\n        self.T = len(obs_samples)\n\n        design_samples = [\n            node\n            for node in main_model_trace.nodes.values()\n            if node.get(\"subtype\") == \"design_sample\"\n        ]\n        if len(design_samples) > 0:\n            # Make sure there are design variables\n            self.design_size = design_samples[0][\"value\"].shape[0]\n\n        y_possible_paths = []\n        for t in range(self.T):\n            expansion = [self.y_support_size] * t\n            tensor = rexpand(obs_support.clone().detach(), *expansion)\n            y_possible_paths.append(tensor)\n        # list of size T; each element is of shape support_size**T\n        y_possible_paths = broadcast_all(*y_possible_paths)\n        # convert the above to a dictionary\n        y_possible_paths = {f\"y{t + 1}\": y_possible_paths[t] for t in range(self.T)}\n\n        self.y_possible_paths = y_possible_paths\n\n    def expand_model_traces(self, model, *args, **kwargs):\n        if not self.y_possible_paths:\n            # compute possible paths just once\n            self.get_all_possible_paths(model, *args, **kwargs)\n\n        y_possible_paths_outer = {\n            k: rexpand(v, self.num_outer_samples)\n            for k, v in self.y_possible_paths.items()\n        }\n        y_possible_paths_inner = {\n            k: rexpand(v, self.num_inner_samples)\n            for k, v in self.y_possible_paths.items()\n        }\n        design_expansion = pyro.condition(\n            self._vectorized(\n                model, *([self.y_support_size] * self.T), name=\"design_expansion\"\n            ),\n            data=self.y_possible_paths,\n        )\n        # sample a design path for each possible y path\n        trace_designs = pyro.poutine.trace(design_expansion).get_trace()\n        trace_designs = prune_subsample_sites(trace_designs)\n        # dict with T elements; each element is of shape [support_size**T, design_size]\n        designs = {\n            name: node[\"value\"]\n            for name, node in trace_designs.nodes.items()\n            if node.get(\"subtype\") == \"design_sample\"\n        }\n        # add new dimension of size <INNER> at position -2 to designs and paths\n        designs_outer = {\n            k: v.unsqueeze(-2).expand(\n                [self.y_support_size] * self.T\n                + [self.num_outer_samples, self.design_size]\n            )\n            for k, v in designs.items()\n        }\n        designs_inner = {\n            k: v.unsqueeze(-2).expand(\n                [self.y_support_size] * self.T\n                + [self.num_inner_samples, self.design_size]\n            )\n            for k, v in designs.items()\n        }\n\n        def outer_vectorization():\n            with pyro.plate(\"outer_vectorization\", self.num_outer_samples):\n                return model()\n\n        def inner_vectorization():\n            with pyro.plate(\"inner_vectorization\", (self.num_inner_samples)):\n                return model()\n\n        trace_pce_primary = pyro.poutine.trace(outer_vectorization).get_trace()\n        trace_pce_contrastive = pyro.poutine.trace(inner_vectorization).get_trace()\n        data_primary = {\n            name: node[\"value\"]\n            for name, node in trace_pce_primary.nodes.items()\n            if node.get(\"subtype\") == \"latent_sample\"\n        }\n        # add the y-paths enumeration to the data dict:\n        data_primary.update(y_possible_paths_outer)\n        data_primary.update(designs_outer)\n\n        data_contrastive = {\n            name: node[\"value\"]\n            for name, node in trace_pce_contrastive.nodes.items()\n            if node.get(\"subtype\") == \"latent_sample\"\n        }\n        data_contrastive.update(y_possible_paths_inner)\n        data_contrastive.update(designs_inner)\n\n        # Expand and condition\n        def expanded_model_primary():\n            with pyro.plate_stack(\n                \"expansion_primary\", [2] * self.T + [self.num_outer_samples]\n            ):\n                # condition on the whole data:\n                return pyro.condition(model, data=data_primary)()\n\n        def expanded_model_contrastive():\n            with pyro.plate_stack(\n                \"expansion_contrastive\", [2] * self.T + [self.num_inner_samples]\n            ):\n                # condition on the whole data:\n                return pyro.condition(model, data=data_contrastive)()\n\n        ## expand primary trace and compute log probs\n        trace_pce_primary = pyro.poutine.trace(expanded_model_primary).get_trace()\n        trace_pce_primary = prune_subsample_sites(trace_pce_primary)\n        # compute the log-probabilities at each site\n        trace_pce_primary.compute_log_prob()\n\n        ## expand Contrastive trace and compute log probs\n        trace_pce_contrastive = pyro.poutine.trace(\n            expanded_model_contrastive\n        ).get_trace()\n        trace_pce_contrastive = prune_subsample_sites(trace_pce_contrastive)\n        # compute the log-probabilities at each site\n        trace_pce_contrastive.compute_log_prob()\n\n        return trace_pce_primary, trace_pce_contrastive\n\n    def differentiable_loss(self, model, *args, **kwargs):\n        \"\"\"\n        Surrogate loss using complete enumeration, which can be differentiated\n        \"\"\"\n        trace_pce_primary, trace_pce_contrastive = self.expand_model_traces(\n            model, args, kwargs\n        )\n        h_T_log_prob_primary = sum(\n            node[\"log_prob\"]\n            for node in trace_pce_primary.nodes.values()\n            if node.get(\"name\") in [f\"y{i}\" for i in range(1, self.T + 1)]\n        )\n        assert h_T_log_prob_primary.shape == torch.Size(\n            [self.y_support_size] * self.T + [self.num_outer_samples]\n        )\n        # entropy of the conditional (numerator); shape is [INNER]\n        # Sum over all paths [entropy of the likelihood, p(h_T|theta, pi)]\n        likelihood_entropy = (-h_T_log_prob_primary * h_T_log_prob_primary.exp()).sum(\n            dim=list(range(0, self.T))\n        )\n        # average the above over theta\n        conditional_entropy = likelihood_entropy.mean(0)\n\n        ## Denominator ###\n        h_T_log_prob_contrastive = sum(\n            node[\"log_prob\"]\n            for node in trace_pce_contrastive.nodes.values()\n            if node.get(\"name\") in [f\"y{i}\" for i in range(1, self.T + 1)]\n        )\n        assert h_T_log_prob_contrastive.shape == torch.Size(\n            [self.y_support_size] * self.T + [self.num_inner_samples]\n        )\n        # \\int p(theta)*p(h_t| theta, pi) which is part of the denom, which is\n        # denom = E_theta\\sum_h_t p(h_t|theta, pi) log(p(h_t|pi)) = \\sum_h_t(..)\n\n        # marg_log_probs = h_T_log_prob_contrastive.logsumexp(-1, keepdims=True)\n        h_T_log_prob_combined = torch.cat(\n            [\n                rexpand(h_T_log_prob_contrastive, self.num_outer_samples),\n                h_T_log_prob_primary.unsqueeze(-2),\n            ],\n            dim=-2,\n        )\n        assert h_T_log_prob_combined.shape == torch.Size(\n            [self.y_support_size] * self.T\n            + [self.num_inner_samples + 1, self.num_outer_samples]\n        )\n        marg_log_probs = h_T_log_prob_combined.logsumexp(-2)\n        # \\sum_h_t marg_log_probs * log (marg_log_probs)\n        marginal_entropy = (\n            -(h_T_log_prob_primary.exp() * marg_log_probs)\n            .sum(dim=list(range(0, self.T)))\n            .mean()\n        )\n        mi_estimate = -conditional_entropy + marginal_entropy\n        loss = -mi_estimate\n        warn_if_nan(loss, \"loss\")\n        return loss\n\n\nclass PriorContrastiveEstimationScoreGradient(PriorContrastiveEstimation):\n    def __init__(self, num_outer_samples, num_inner_samples):\n      with torch.no_grad():\n            g_no_grad = obs_log_prob_primary - obs_log_prob_combined\n\n        loss = -(g_no_grad * obs_log_prob_primary - obs_log_prob_combined).mean(0)\n\n rmv(A, b):\n    \"\"\"Tensorized matrix vector multiplication of rightmost dimensions.\"\"\"\n    return torch.matmul(A, b.unsqueeze(-1)).squeeze(-1)\n\n\nclass LazyDelta(Delta):\n    def __init__(self, fn, prototype, log_density=0.0, event_dim=0, validate_args=None):\n        self.fn = fn\n        super().__init__(\n            prototype,\n            log_density=log_density,\n            event_dim=event_dim,\n            validate_args=validate_args,\n        )\n\n    def expand(self, batch_shape, _instance=None):\n        new = self._get_checked_instance(LazyDelta, _instance)\n        new.fn = self.fn\n        batch_shape = torch.Size(batch_shape)\n        new.v = self.v.expand(batch_shape + self.event_shape)\n        new.log_density = self.log_density.expand(batch_shape)\n        super(Delta, new).__init__(batch_shape, self.event_shape, validate_args=False)\n        new._validate_args = self._validate_args\n        return new\n\n    def rsample(self, sample_shape=torch.Size()):\n        # The shape of self.v will have expanded along with any .expand calls\n        shape = sample_shape + self.v.shape\n        output = self.fn()\n        return output.expand(shape)\n\n    @property\n    def variance(self):\n        return torch.zeros_like(self.v)\n\n    def log_prob(self, x):\n        return self.log_density\n\n\nclass LazyFn:\n    def __init__(self, f, prototype):\n        self.f = f\n        self.prototype = prototype.clone()\n\n    def lazy(self, *design_obs_pairs):\n        def delayed_function():\n            return  delayed_function, self.prototype, event_dim=self.prototype.dim()\n        )\n        return lazy_delta\n\n    def forward(self, *design_obs_pairs):\n        if len(design_obs_pairs) == 0:\n   self.designs = nn.ParameterList(\n            [nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(design_dim)) for i in range(T)]\n        )\n\n    def forward(self, *design_obs_pairs):\n        j = len(design_obs_pairs)\n        return self.designs[j]\n\n\nclass EncoderNetwork(nn.Module):\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        design_dim,\n        osbervation_dim,\n        hidden_dim,\n        encoding_dim,\n        n_hidden_layers=2,\n        activation=nn.Softplus,\n    ):\n        super().__init__()\n        self.encoding_dim = encoding_dim\n        self.activation_layer = activation()\n nn.Sequential(nn.Linear(hidden_dim, hidden_dim), activation())\n                    for _ in range(n_hidden_layers - 1)\n                ]\n            )\n        else:\n            self.middle = self.input_layer(inputs)\n        x = self.activation_layer(x)\n        x = self.middle(x)\n        x = self.output_layer(x)\n        return x\n\n\nclass EmitterNetwork(nn.Module):\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        input_dim,\n        hidden_dim,\n        output_dim,\n        n_hidden_layers=2,\n        activation=nn.Softplus,\n    ):\n        super().__init__()\n        self.activation_layer = activation()\n        self.input_layer = nn.Linear(input_dim, hidden_dim)\n        if n_hidden_layers > 1:\n            self.middle = nn.Sequential(\n                *[\n                    nn.Sequential(nn.Linear(hidden_dim, hidden_dim), activation())\n                    for _ in range(n_hidden_layers - 1)\n                ]\n            )\n        else:\n            self.middle = nn.Identity()\n        self.output_layer = nn.Linear(hidden_dim, output_dim)\n\n    def forward(self, r):\n        x = self.input_layer(r)\n        x = self.activation_layer(x)\n        x = self.middle(x)\n        x = self.output_layer(x)\n        return x\n\n\nimport torch\nimport pyro\nfrom pyro.infer.util import torch_item\n\n\nclass OED:\n    def __init__(self, model, optim, loss, **kwargs):\n\n        self.model = model\n        self.optim = optim\n        self.loss = loss\n        super().__init__(**kwargs)\n\n        if not isinstance(optim, pyro.optim.PyroOptim):\n            raise ValueError(\n                \"Optimizer should be an instance of pyro.optim.PyroOptim class.\"\n            )\n\n    def evaluate_loss(self, *args, **kwargs):\n        \"\"\"\n        :returns: estimate of the loss\n        :rtype: float\n        Evaluate the loss function. Any args or kwargs are passed to the model and guide.\n        \"\"\"\n        with torch.no_grad():\n            loss = self.loss.loss(self.model, *args, **kwargs)\n            if isinstance(loss, tuple):\n                # Support losses that return a tuple\n                return type(loss)(map(torch_item, loss))\n            else:\n                return torch_item(loss)\n\n    def step(self, clip_grads=True, *args, **kwargs):\n        \"\"\"\n        :returns: estimate of the loss\n        :rtype: float\n        Take a gradient step on the loss function (and any auxiliary loss functions\n        generated under the hood by `loss_and_grads`).\n        Any args or kwargs are passed to the model and guide\n        \"\"\"\n\n        with pyro.poutine.trace(param_only=True) as param_capture:\n            loss = self.loss.differentiable_loss(self.model, *args, **kwargs)\n            loss.backward()\n\n        params = set(\n            site[\"value\"].unconstrained() for site in param_capture.trace.nodes.values()\n        )\n        # gradient clipping: The norm is computed over all gradients together,\n        # as if they were concatenated into a single vector.\n        if clip_grads:\n            torch.nn.utils.clip_grad_norm_(params, max_norm=1.0, norm_type=\"inf\")\n\n        # actually perform gradient steps\n        # torch.optim objects gets instantiated for any params that haven't been seen yet\n        self.optim(params)\n\n        # zero gradients\n        pyro.infer.util.zero_grads(params)\n\n        if isinstance(loss, tuple):\n            # Support losses that return a tuple, e.g. ReweightedWakeSleep.\n            return type(loss)(map(torch_item, loss))\n        else:\n            return torch_item(loss)\n\n\nimport warnings\n\nfrom pyro.poutine.runtime import am_i_wrapped, apply_stack\n\n\ndef sample_with_type(type_string, name, fn, *args, **kwargs):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calls the stochastic function `fn` with additional side-effects depending\n    on `name` and the enclosing context (e.g. an inference algorithm).\n    See `Intro I <http://pyro.ai/examples/intro_part_i.html>`_ and\n    `Intro II <http://pyro.ai/examples/intro_part_ii.html>`_ for a discussion.\n    :param type_string: annotate this site with a particular type\n    :param name: name of sample\n    :param fn: distribution class or function\n    :param obs: observed datum (optional; should only be used in context of\n        inference) optionally specified in kwargs\n    :param dict infer: Optional dictionary of inference parameters specified\n        in kwargs. See inference documentation for details.\n    :returns: sample\n    \"\"\"\n    obs = kwargs.pop(\"obs\", None)\n    infer = kwargs.pop(\"infer\", {}).copy()\n    # check if stack is empty\n    # if stack empty, default behavior (defined here)\n    if not am_i_wrapped():\n        if obs is not None and not infer.get(\"_deterministic\"):\n            warnings.warn(\n                \"trying to observe a value outside of inference at \" + name,\n                RuntimeWarning,\n            )\n            return obs\n        return fn(*args, **kwargs)\n    # if stack not empty, apply everything in the stack?\n    else:\n        # initialize data structure to pass up/down the stack\n        msg = {\n            \"type\": \"sample\",\n            \"subtype\": type_string,\n            \"name\": name,\n            \"fn\": fn,\n            \"is_observed\": False,\n            \"args\": args,\n            \"kwargs\": kwargs,\n            \"value\": None,\n            \"infer\": infer,\n            \"scale\": 1.0,\n            \"mask\": None,\n            \"cond_indep_stack\": (),n            \"done\": False,\n            \"stop\": False,\n            \"continuation\": None,\n        }\n        # handle observation\n        if obs is not None:\n            msg[\"value\"] = obs\n            msg[\"is_observed\"] = True\n        # apply the stack and return its return value\n        apply_stack(msg)\n        return msg[\"value\"]\n\n\ndef observation_sample(name, fn, *args, **kwargs):\n    return sample_with_type(\"observation_sample\", name, fn, *args, **kwargs)\n\n\ndef compute_design(name, fn, *args, **kwargs):\n    return sample_with_type(\"design_sample\", name, fn, *args, **kwargs)\n\n\ndef latent_sample(name, fn, *args, **kwargs):\n    return sample_with_type(\"latent_sample\", name, fn, *args, **kwargs)\n\n\nimport torch\nfrom torch import nn\nimport pyro.distributions as dist\nfrom oed.primitives import observation_sample, compute_design\n\nclass DeathProcess(nn.Module):\n    \"\"\"Model class for Death Process experiment.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        design_net,\n        theta_loc=None,\n        theta_scale=None,\n        theta_dist=\"truncated normal\",\n        N=50,\n        T=2,\n    ):\n        super().__init__()\n        self.design_net = design_net\n        self.theta_loc = theta_loc if theta_loc is not None else torch.tensor(1.0)\n        self.theta_scale = theta_scale if theta_scale is not None else torch.tensor(1.0)\n        if theta_dist == \"truncated normal\":\n            from extra_distributions.truncated_normal import LowerTruncatedNormal\n            self.theta_prior_dist = LowerTruncatedNormal(\n                self.theta_loc, self.theta_scale, 0.0\n            )\n        elif theta_dist == \"lognormal\":\n            self.theta_prior_dist = dist.LogNormal(self.theta_loc, self.theta_scale)\n        else:\n            raise ValueError(\"Invalid option: `theta_dist`=%s.\" % theta_dist)\n        self.T = T\n        self.N = N\n        self.softplus = nn.Softplus()\n\n    def model(self):\n        if hasattr(self.design_net, \"parameters\"):\n            pyro.module(\"design_net\", self.design_net)\n\n        ########################################################################\n        # Sample latent variables\n        ########################################################################\n        theta = pyro.sample(\"theta\", self.theta_prior_dist)\n        theta = theta.clamp(min=1e-10, max=1e10)\n\n        y_outcomes = []\n        xi_designs = []\n\n        for t in range(self.T):\n            ####################################################################\n            # Get a design xi\n            ####################################################################\n            xi = compute_design(\n                f\"xi{t + 1}\", self.design_net.lazy(*zip(xi_designs, y_outcomes))\n            )\n            xi = self.softplus(xi.squeeze(-1))\n\n            ####################################################################\n            # Sample y\n            ####################################################################\n            death_probability = 1 - (-xi * theta).exp()\n            y = observation_sample(\n                f\"y{t + 1}\", dist.Binomial(total_count=self.N, probs=death_probability)\n            )\n            y_outcomes.append(y)\n            xi_designs.append(xi)\n\n        return y_outcomes",
    "Experiment Result": "- **General Training Parameters:**\n  - `seed`: -1 (for random seeding)\n  - `num_steps`: 5000 (number of optimization steps)\n  - `lr`: 0.001 (learning rate for Adam optimizer)\n  - `gamma`: 0.95 (learning rate scheduler decay factor)\n  - `device`: 'cuda' (or 'cpu')\n  - `mlflow_experiment_name`: 'Default'\n\n- **DAD (Deep Adaptive Design) Specific Parameters:**\n  - `num_inner_samples`: 100 (L in denominator of sPCE loss, number of inner samples for contrastive estimation)\n  - `num_outer_samples`: 200 (N for estimating outer expectation of sPCE loss)\n  - `complete_enum`: False (determines whether to use `PriorContrastiveEstimationDiscreteObsTotalEnum` for complete enumeration or `PriorContrastiveEstimationScoreGradient` for score gradient estimation. Default uses score gradient)\n\n- **Design Network Architecture Parameters (`SetEquivariantDesignNetwork`):**\n  - `arch`: 'sum' (architecture type, 'sum' implies `SetEquivariantDesignNetwork` with pooling by summation using `EncoderNetwork` and `EmitterNetwork`)\n  - `hidden_dim`: 128 (hidden dimension for encoder and emitter networks)\n  - `encoding_dim`: 16 (output dimension of the encoder network)\n  - `num_layers`: 2 (number of hidden layers in encoder and emitter networks)\n\n- **Death Process Model Parameters:**\n  - `T`: 4 (num_experiments, number of sequential experimental stages)\n  - `N`: 50 (num_people, number of subjects in each experiment, determining `total_count` for Binomial observation)\n  - `theta_prior_dist`: LowerTruncatedNormal(loc=1.0, scale=1.0, truncation=0.0) (prior distribution for the latent variable `theta`)\n"

    "Title": "Deep Adaptive Design: Amortizing Sequential Bayesian Experimental Design",
    "Main Contributions": "The paper introduces Deep Adaptive Design (DAD), a novel method that amortizes the computational cost of adaptive Bayesian optimal experimental design (BOED) to enable real-time execution of experiments. Traditionally, sequential BOED requires substantial computation at each stage, making it impractical for applications needing rapid decisions. DAD addresses this by learning an amortized design network upfront, which then rapidly outputs the next design using a single forward pass (in milliseconds). Key contributions include reformulating the sequential BOED problem into a single holistic objective based on overall expected information gain, deriving contrastive information bounds (sPCE) suitable for sequential settings for end-to-end policy training, and proposing a customized neural network architecture that exploits key permutation symmetries of the problem. DAD is shown to successfully amortize experimental design, often outperforming conventional, non-amortized adaptive BOED methods, and is capable of learning non-myopic adaptive strategies.",
    "Methodology": "DAD employs a neural network, called the design network (πφ), to represent a design policy. This network takes the history of previous design-observation pairs as input and outputs the next optimal design. The network is trained upfront by simulating hypothetical experimental trajectories to learn near-optimal design decisions. The core objective, IT(π), maximizes the total expected information gained from the entire sequence of T experiments, representing the mutual information between the unknown latent parameters (θ) and the full history (hT). To optimize this intractable objective, DAD introduces contrastive lower bounds, specifically Sequential Prior Contrastive Estimation (sPCE), which allow for end-to-end training using stochastic gradient ascent without direct posterior or marginal likelihood estimation. For gradient estimation, the reparametrization trick is used for continuous, reparametrizable likelihoods, and the score function gradient estimator (REINFORCE) for discrete observations or non-reparametrizable likelihoods. The architecture exploits permutation invariance by using an encoder network (Eφ1) to create a pooled, fixed-dimensional representation of the history, which is then fed into an emitter network (Fφ2) to output the design.",
    "Experimental Setup": "The DAD method was implemented using PyTorch and Pyro, with code made publicly available. It was compared against several baselines: random design, a fixed (static) design optimized by SG-BOED, a variational baseline (SG-BOED used iteratively to approximate the optimal myopic policy), and problem-specific adaptive strategies (e.g., Kirby (2009), Frye et al. (2016), Badapted from Vincent & Rainforth (2017), and SeqBED from Kleinegesse et al. (2020)). Performance was evaluated primarily on total Expected Information Gain (EIG) using sPCE lower bounds and sNMC upper bounds, and critically, on deployment time (the time to run the experiment itself after pre-training). Experiments were conducted on: 1) Location finding in 2D (finding K=2 hidden sources over T=30 experiments), 2) Hyperbolic temporal discounting (T=20 binary questions to model human decision-making in psychology), and 3) a Death process model (T=4 sequential observations in epidemiology). Deployment speed tests were performed on lightweight CPU-only machines to mimic real-world deployment conditions. Ablation studies included testing training stability across multiple runs and the ability to generalize to varying sequence lengths, as well as comparing different gradient estimation methods for discrete observations.",
    "Limitations": "The current form of DAD has several restrictions. Firstly, it requires the likelihood model to be explicit and evaluable (p(yt|θ,ξt)). Secondly, it assumes that experiments are conditionally independent given θ, meaning p(y1:T|θ,ξ1:T) = ∏T t=1 p(yt|θ,ξt), which makes it unsuitable for models with inherent sequential dependencies like time series models. Thirdly, the designs (ξt) must be continuous to facilitate gradient-based optimization. Lastly, while DAD often outperforms non-amortized methods, the reason is attributed to avoiding posterior approximation errors and enabling non-myopic policies, rather than a guaranteed theoretical superiority for all problems.",
    "Future Research Directions": "Future work could focus on addressing the identified limitations, such as extending DAD to handle implicit likelihood models where p(yt|θ,ξt) cannot be explicitly evaluated. Another direction is to adapt the framework to scenarios with conditionally dependent experiments, like time series models, where the current assumption of independence given θ does not hold. Developing methods for discrete design spaces, rather than being limited to continuous designs for gradient-based optimization, is also a promising avenue. Furthermore, the paper suggests exploring deeper connections between DAD and model-based reinforcement learning, despite the absence of observed rewards in experimental design, to potentially leverage insights from that field.",
    "Experiment Code": "import math\n\nimport torch\nfrom torch.distributions.utils import broadcast_all\n\nimport pyro\nfrom pyro import poutine\nfrom pyro.poutine.util import prune_subsample_sites\nfrom pyro.util import warn_if_nan\nfrom pyro.infer.util import torch_item\nfrom pyro.contrib.util import lexpand, rexpand\n\n\nclass MutualInformation(object):\n    def __init__(self, num_outer_samples, data_source=None):\n        self.data_source = data_source\n        self.num_outer_samples = num_outer_samples\n\n    def _vectorized(self, fn, *shape, name=\"vectorization_plate\"):\n        \"\"\"\n        Wraps a callable inside an outermost :class:`~pyro.plate` to parallelize\n        MI computation over `num_particles`, and to broadcast batch shapes of\n        sample site functions in accordance with the `~pyro.plate` contexts\n        within which they are embedded.\n        :param fn: arbitrary callable containing Pyro primitives.\n        :return: wrapped callable.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        def wrapped_fn(*args, **kwargs):\n            with pyro.plate_stack(name, shape):\n                return fn(*args, **kwargs)\n\n        return wrapped_fn\n\n    def get_primary_rollout(self, model, args, kwargs, graph_type=\"flat\", detach=False):\n        if self.data_source is None:\n            model = self._vectorized(\n                model, self.num_outer_samples, name=\"outer_vectorization\"\n            )\n        else:\n            data = next(self.data_source)\n            model = pyro.condition(\n                self._vectorized(\n                    model, self.num_outer_samples, name=\"outer_vectorization\"\n                ),\n                data=data,\n            )\n\n        trace = poutine.trace(model, graph_type=graph_type).get_trace(*args, **kwargs)\n        if detach:\n            trace.detach_()\n        trace = prune_subsample_sites(trace)\n        trace.compute_log_prob()\n        return trace\n\n\nclass PriorContrastiveEstimation(MutualInformation):\n    def __init__(self, num_outer_samples=100, num_inner_samples=10, data_source=None):\n        super().__init__(num_outer_samples, data_source=data_source)\n        self.num_inner_samples = num_inner_samples\n\n    def compute_observation_log_prob(self, trace):\n        \"\"\"\n        Computes the log probability of observations given latent variables and designs.\n        :param trace: a Pyro trace object\n        :return: the log prob tensor\n        \"\"\"\n        return sum(\n            node[\"log_prob\"]\n            for node in trace.nodes.values()\n            if node.get(\"subtype\") == \"observation_sample\"\n        )\n\n    def get_contrastive_rollout(\n        self,\n        trace,\n        model,\n        args,\n        kwargs,\n        existing_vectorization,\n        graph_type=\"flat\",\n        detach=False,\n    ):\n        sampled_observation_values = {\n            name: lexpand(node[\"value\"], self.num_inner_samples)\n            for name, node in trace.nodes.items()\n            if node.get(\"subtype\") in [\"observation_sample\", \"design_sample\"]\n        }\n        conditional_model = self._vectorized(\n            pyro.condition(model, data=sampled_observation_values),\n            self.num_inner_samples,\n            *existing_vectorization,\n            name=\"inner_vectorization\",\n        )\n        trace = poutine.trace(conditional_model, graph_type=graph_type).get_trace(\n            *args, **kwargs\n        )\n        if detach:\n            trace.detach_()\n        trace = prune_subsample_sites(trace)\n        trace.compute_log_prob()\n\n        return trace\n\n    def differentiable_loss(self, model, *args, **kwargs):\n        \"\"\"\n        Computes the surrogate loss that can be differentiated with autograd\n        to produce gradient estimates for the design\n        \"\"\"\n        primary_trace = self.get_primary_rollout(model, args, kwargs)\n        contrastive_trace = self.get_contrastive_rollout(\n            primary_trace, model, args, kwargs, [self.num_outer_samples]\n        )\n\n        obs_log_prob_primary = self.compute_observation_log_prob(primary_trace)\n        obs_log_prob_contrastive = self.compute_observation_log_prob(contrastive_trace)\n\n        obs_log_prob_combined = torch.cat(\n            [lexpand(obs_log_prob_primary, 1), obs_log_prob_contrastive]\n        ).logsumexp(0)\n\n        loss = (obs_log_prob_combined - obs_log_prob_primary).mean(0)\n\n        warn_if_nan(loss, \"loss\")\n        return loss\n\n    def loss(self, model, *args, **kwargs):\n        \"\"\"\n        :returns: returns an estimate of the mutual information\n        :rtype: float\n        Evaluates the MI lower bound using prior contrastive estimation; == -EIG proxy\n        \"\"\"\n        loss_to_constant = torch_item(self.differentiable_loss(model, *args, **kwargs))\n        return loss_to_constant - math.log(self.num_inner_samples + 1)\n\n\nclass PriorContrastiveEstimationDiscreteObsTotalEnum(PriorContrastiveEstimation):\n    def __init__(self, num_outer_samples, num_inner_samples, data_source=None):\n        super().__init__(\n            num_outer_samples=num_outer_samples,\n            num_inner_samples=num_inner_samples,\n            data_source=data_source,\n        )\n        self.T = None\n        self.design_size = None\n        self.y_support_size = None\n        self.y_possible_paths = None\n\n    def get_all_possible_paths(self, model, *args, **kwargs):\n        \"\"\"Expand histories\"\"\"\n        main_model_trace = pyro.poutine.trace(model).get_trace()\n        obs_samples = [\n            node\n            for node in main_model_trace.nodes.values()\n            if node.get(\"subtype\") == \"observation_sample\"\n        ]\n        obs_support = obs_samples[0][\"fn\"].enumerate_support()\n        self.y_support_size = obs_support.shape[0]  # e.g for hyperbolic temp = 2\n        self.T = len(obs_samples)\n\n        design_samples = [\n            node\n            for node in main_model_trace.nodes.values()\n            if node.get(\"subtype\") == \"design_sample\"\n        ]\n        if len(design_samples) > 0:\n            # Make sure there are design variables\n            self.design_size = design_samples[0][\"value\"].shape[0]\n\n        y_possible_paths = []\n        for t in range(self.T):\n            expansion = [self.y_support_size] * t\n            tensor = rexpand(obs_support.clone().detach(), *expansion)\n            y_possible_paths.append(tensor)\n        # list of size T; each element is of shape support_size**T\n        y_possible_paths = broadcast_all(*y_possible_paths)\n        # convert the above to a dictionary\n        y_possible_paths = {f\"y{t + 1}\": y_possible_paths[t] for t in range(self.T)}\n\n        self.y_possible_paths = y_possible_paths\n\n    def expand_model_traces(self, model, *args, **kwargs):\n        if not self.y_possible_paths:\n            # compute possible paths just once\n            self.get_all_possible_paths(model, *args, **kwargs)\n\n        y_possible_paths_outer = {\n            k: rexpand(v, self.num_outer_samples)\n            for k, v in self.y_possible_paths.items()\n        }\n        y_possible_paths_inner = {\n            k: rexpand(v, self.num_inner_samples)\n            for k, v in self.y_possible_paths.items()\n        }\n        design_expansion = pyro.condition(\n            self._vectorized(\n                model, *([self.y_support_size] * self.T), name=\"design_expansion\"\n            ),\n            data=self.y_possible_paths,\n        )\n        # sample a design path for each possible y path\n        trace_designs = pyro.poutine.trace(design_expansion).get_trace()\n        trace_designs = prune_subsample_sites(trace_designs)\n        # dict with T elements; each element is of shape [support_size**T, design_size]\n        designs = {\n            name: node[\"value\"]\n            for name, node in trace_designs.nodes.items()\n            if node.get(\"subtype\") == \"design_sample\"\n        }\n        # add new dimension of size <INNER> at position -2 to designs and paths\n        designs_outer = {\n            k: v.unsqueeze(-2).expand(\n                [self.y_support_size] * self.T\n                + [self.num_outer_samples, self.design_size]\n            )\n            for k, v in designs.items()\n        }\n        designs_inner = {\n            k: v.unsqueeze(-2).expand(\n                [self.y_support_size] * self.T\n                + [self.num_inner_samples, self.design_size]\n            )\n            for k, v in designs.items()\n        }\n\n        def outer_vectorization():\n            with pyro.plate(\"outer_vectorization\", self.num_outer_samples):\n                return model()\n\n        def inner_vectorization():\n            with pyro.plate(\"inner_vectorization\", (self.num_inner_samples)):\n                return model()\n\n        trace_pce_primary = pyro.poutine.trace(outer_vectorization).get_trace()\n        trace_pce_contrastive = pyro.poutine.trace(inner_vectorization).get_trace()\n        data_primary = {\n            name: node[\"value\"]\n            for name, node in trace_pce_primary.nodes.items()\n            if node.get(\"subtype\") == \"latent_sample\"\n        }\n        # add the y-paths enumeration to the data dict:\n        data_primary.update(y_possible_paths_outer)\n        data_primary.update(designs_outer)\n\n        data_contrastive = {\n            name: node[\"value\"]\n            for name, node in trace_pce_contrastive.nodes.items()\n            if node.get(\"subtype\") == \"latent_sample\"\n        }\n        data_contrastive.update(y_possible_paths_inner)\n        data_contrastive.update(designs_inner)\n\n        # Expand and condition\n        def expanded_model_primary():\n            with pyro.plate_stack(\n                \"expansion_primary\", [2] * self.T + [self.num_outer_samples]\n            ):\n                # condition on the whole data:\n                return pyro.condition(model, data=data_primary)()\n\n        def expanded_model_contrastive():\n            with pyro.plate_stack(\n                \"expansion_contrastive\", [2] * self.T + [self.num_inner_samples]\n            ):\n                # condition on the whole data:\n                return pyro.condition(model, data=data_contrastive)()\n\n        ## expand primary trace and compute log probs\n        trace_pce_primary = pyro.poutine.trace(expanded_model_primary).get_trace()\n        trace_pce_primary = prune_subsample_sites(trace_pce_primary)\n        # compute the log-probabilities at each site\n        trace_pce_primary.compute_log_prob()\n\n        ## expand Contrastive trace and compute log probs\n        trace_pce_contrastive = pyro.poutine.trace(\n            expanded_model_contrastive\n        ).get_trace()\n        trace_pce_contrastive = prune_subsample_sites(trace_pce_contrastive)\n        # compute the log-probabilities at each site\n        trace_pce_contrastive.compute_log_prob()\n\n        return trace_pce_primary, trace_pce_contrastive\n\n    def differentiable_loss(self, model, *args, **kwargs):\n        \"\"\"\n        Surrogate loss using complete enumeration, which can be differentiated\n        \"\"\"\n        trace_pce_primary, trace_pce_contrastive = self.expand_model_traces(\n            model, args, kwargs\n        )\n        h_T_log_prob_primary = sum(\n            node[\"log_prob\"]\n            for node in trace_pce_primary.nodes.values()\n            if node.get(\"name\") in [f\"y{i}\" for i in range(1, self.T + 1)]\n        )\n        assert h_T_log_prob_primary.shape == torch.Size(\n            [self.y_support_size] * self.T + [self.num_outer_samples]\n        )\n        # entropy of the conditional (numerator); shape is [INNER]\n        # Sum over all paths [entropy of the likelihood, p(h_T|theta, pi)]\n        likelihood_entropy = (-h_T_log_prob_primary * h_T_log_prob_primary.exp()).sum(\n            dim=list(range(0, self.T))\n        )\n        # average the above over theta\n        conditional_entropy = likelihood_entropy.mean(0)\n\n        ## Denominator ###\n        h_T_log_prob_contrastive = sum(\n            node[\"log_prob\"]\n            for node in trace_pce_contrastive.nodes.values()\n            if node.get(\"name\") in [f\"y{i}\" for i in range(1, self.T + 1)]\n        )\n        assert h_T_log_prob_contrastive.shape == torch.Size(\n            [self.y_support_size] * self.T + [self.num_inner_samples]\n        )\n        # \\int p(theta)*p(h_t| theta, pi) which is part of the denom, which is\n        # denom = E_theta\\sum_h_t p(h_t|theta, pi) log(p(h_t|pi)) = \\sum_h_t(..)\n\n        # marg_log_probs = h_T_log_prob_contrastive.logsumexp(-1, keepdims=True)\n        h_T_log_prob_combined = torch.cat(\n            [\n                rexpand(h_T_log_prob_contrastive, self.num_outer_samples),\n                h_T_log_prob_primary.unsqueeze(-2),\n            ],\n            dim=-2,\n        )\n        assert h_T_log_prob_combined.shape == torch.Size(\n            [self.y_support_size] * self.T\n            + [self.num_inner_samples + 1, self.num_outer_samples]\n        )\n        marg_log_probs = h_T_log_prob_combined.logsumexp(-2)\n        # \\sum_h_t marg_log_probs * log (marg_log_probs)\n        marginal_entropy =\n            -(h_T_log_prob_primary.exp() * marg_log_probs)\n            .sum(dim=list(range(0, self.T)))\n            .mean()\n        mi_estimate = -conditional_entropy + marginal_entropy\n        loss = -mi_estimate\n        warn_if_nan(loss, \"loss\")\n        return loss\n\n\nclass PriorContrastiveEstimationScoreGradient(PriorContrastiveEstimation):\n    def __init__(self, num_outer_samples, num_inner_samples):\n        super().__init__(\n            num_outer_samples=num_outer_samples, num_inner_samples=num_inner_samples\n        )\n\n    def differentiable_loss(self, model, *args, **kwargs):\n        \"\"\"\n        Computes the surrogate loss that can be differentiated with autograd\n        to produce gradient estimates for the design\n        -> log p(h_T|theta, pi_phi)* const(g_phi) + g_phi\n        \"\"\"\n        primary_trace = self.get_primary_rollout(model, args, kwargs)\n        contrastive_trace = self.get_contrastive_rollout(\n            primary_trace, model, args, kwargs, [self.num_outer_samples]\n        )\n\n        obs_log_prob_primary = self.compute_observation_log_prob(primary_trace)\n        obs_log_prob_contrastive = self.compute_observation_log_prob(contrastive_trace)\n        obs_log_prob_combined = torch.cat(\n            [lexpand(obs_log_prob_primary, 1), obs_log_prob_contrastive]\n        ).logsumexp(0)\n\n        with torch.no_grad():\n            g_no_grad = obs_log_prob_primary - obs_log_prob_combined\n\n        loss = -(g_no_grad * obs_log_prob_primary - obs_log_prob_combined).mean(0)\n\n        warn_if_nan(loss, \"loss\")\n        return loss\n\n    def loss(self, model, *args, **kwargs):\n        \"\"\"\n        :returns: returns an estimate of the mutual information\n        :rtype: float\n        Evaluates the MI lower bound using prior contrastive estimation\n        \"\"\"\n        basic_pce = super().differentiable_loss(model, *args, **kwargs)\n        loss_to_constant = torch_item(basic_pce)\n        loss = loss_to_constant - math.log(self.num_inner_samples + 1)\n        return loss\n\nimport torch\nfrom torch import nn\n\nfrom pyro.distributions import Delta\n\n\ndef rmv(A, b):\n    \"\"\"Tensorized matrix vector multiplication of rightmost dimensions.\"\"\"\n    return torch.matmul(A, b.unsqueeze(-1)).squeeze(-1)\n\n\nclass LazyDelta(Delta):\n    def __init__(self, fn, prototype, log_density=0.0, event_dim=0, validate_args=None):\n        self.fn = fn\n        super().__init__(\n            prototype,\n            log_density=log_density,\n            event_dim=event_dim,\n            validate_args=validate_args,\n        )\n\n    def expand(self, batch_shape, _instance=None):\n        new = self._get_checked_instance(LazyDelta, _instance)\n        new.fn = self.fn\n        batch_shape = torch.Size(batch_shape)\n        new.v = self.v.expand(batch_shape + self.event_shape)\n        new.log_density = self.log_density.expand(batch_shape)\n        super(Delta, new).__init__(batch_shape, self.event_shape, validate_args=False)\n        new._validate_args = self._validate_args\n        return new\n\n    def rsample(self, sample_shape=torch.Size()):\n        # The shape of self.v will have expanded along with any .expand calls\n        shape = sample_shape + self.v.shape\n        output = self.fn()\n        return output.expand(shape)\n\n    @property\n    def variance(self):\n        return torch.zeros_like(self.v)\n\n    def log_prob(self, x):\n        return self.log_density\n\n\nclass LazyFn:\n    def __init__(self, f, prototype):\n        self.f = f\n        self.prototype = prototype.clone()\n\n    def lazy(self, *design_obs_pairs):\n        def delayed_function():\n            return self.f(*design_obs_pairs)\n\n        lazy_delta = LazyDelta(\n            delayed_function, self.prototype, event_dim=self.prototype.dim()\n        )\n        return lazy_delta\n\n\nclass SetEquivariantDesignNetwork(nn.Module):\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        encoder_network,\n        emission_network,\n        empty_value,\n    ):\n        super().__init__()\n        self.encoder = encoder_network\n        self.emitter = emission_network\n        self.register_buffer(\"prototype\", empty_value.clone())\n        self.register_parameter(\"empty_value\", nn.Parameter(empty_value))\n\n    def lazy(self, *design_obs_pairs):\n        def delayed_function():\n            return self.forward(*design_obs_pairs)\n\n        lazy_delta = LazyDelta(\n            delayed_function, self.prototype, event_dim=self.prototype.dim()\n        )\n        return lazy_delta\n\n    def forward(self, *design_obs_pairs):\n        if len(design_obs_pairs) == 0:\n            sum_encoding = self.empty_value.new_zeros(self.encoder.encoding_dim)\n\n        else:\n            sum_encoding = sum(\n                self.encoder(xi=design, y=obs, t=[idx + 1])\n                for idx, (design, obs) in enumerate(design_obs_pairs)\n            )\n        output = self.emitter(sum_encoding)\n\n        return output\n\nimport torch\nimport pyro\nfrom pyro.infer.util import torch_item\n\n\nclass OED:\n    def __init__(self, model, optim, loss, **kwargs):\n\n        self.model = model\n        self.optim = optim\n        self.loss = loss\n        super().__init__(**kwargs)\n\n        if not isinstance(optim, pyro.optim.PyroOptim):\n            raise ValueError(\n                \"Optimizer should be an instance of pyro.optim.PyroOptim class.\"\n            )\n\n    def evaluate_loss(self, *args, **kwargs):\n        \"\"\"\n        :returns: estimate of the loss\n        :rtype: float\n        Evaluate the loss function. Any args or kwargs are passed to the model and guide.\n        \"\"\"\n        with torch.no_grad():\n            loss = self.loss.loss(self.model, *args, **kwargs)\n            if isinstance(loss, tuple):\n                # Support losses that return a tuple\n                return type(loss)(map(torch_item, loss))\n            else:\n                return torch_item(loss)\n\n    def step(self, clip_grads=True, *args, **kwargs):\n        \"\"\"\n        :returns: estimate of the loss\n        :rtype: float\n        Take a gradient step on the loss function (and any auxiliary loss functions\n        generated under the hood by `loss_and_grads`).\n        Any args or kwargs are passed to the model and guide\n        \"\"\"\n\n        with pyro.poutine.trace(param_only=True) as param_capture:\n            loss = self.loss.differentiable_loss(self.model, *args, **kwargs)\n            loss.backward()\n\n        params = set(\n            site[\"value\"].unconstrained() for site in param_capture.trace.nodes.values()\n        )\n        # gradient clipping: The norm is computed over all gradients together,\n        # as if they were concatenated into a single vector.\n        if clip_grads:\n            torch.nn.utils.clip_grad_norm_(params, max_norm=1.0, norm_type=\"inf\")\n\n        # actually perform gradient steps\n        # torch.optim objects gets instantiated for any params that haven't been seen yet\n        self.optim(params)\n\n        # zero gradients\n        pyro.infer.util.zero_grads(params)\n\n        if isinstance(loss, tuple):\n            # Support losses that return a tuple, e.g. ReweightedWakeSleep.\n            return type(loss)(map(torch_item, loss))\n        else:\n            return torch_item(loss)\n\nimport warnings\n\nfrom pyro.poutine.runtime import am_i_wrapped, apply_stack\n\n\ndef sample_with_type(type_string, name, fn, *args, **kwargs):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calls the stochastic function `fn` with additional side-effects depending\n    on `name` and the enclosing context (e.g. an inference algorithm).\n    See `Intro I <http://pyro.ai/examples/intro_part_i.html>`_ and\n    `Intro II <http://pyro.ai/examples/intro_part_ii.html>`_ for a discussion.\n    :param type_string: annotate this site with a particular type\n    :param name: name of sample\n    :param fn: distribution class or function\n    :param obs: observed datum (optional; should only be used in context of\n        inference) optionally specified in kwargs\n    :param dict infer: Optional dictionary of inference parameters specified\n        in kwargs. See inference documentation for details.\n    :returns: sample\n    \"\"\"\n    obs = kwargs.pop(\"obs\", None)\n    infer = kwargs.pop(\"infer\", {}).copy()\n    # check if stack is empty\n    # if stack empty, default behavior (defined here)\n    if not am_i_wrapped():\n        if obs is not None and not infer.get(\"_deterministic\"):\n            warnings.warn(\n                \"trying to observe a value outside of inference at \" + name,\n                RuntimeWarning,\n            )\n            return obs\n        return fn(*args, **kwargs)\n    # if stack not empty, apply everything in the stack?\n    else:\n        # initialize data structure to pass up/down the stack\n        msg = {\n            \"type\": \"sample\",\n            \"subtype\": type_string,\n            \"name\": name,\n            \"fn\": fn,\n            \"is_observed\": False,\n            \"args\": args,\n            \"kwargs\": kwargs,\n            \"value\": None,\n            \"infer\": infer,\n            \"scale\": 1.0,\n            \"mask\": None,\n            \"cond_indep_stack\": (),\n            \"done\": False,\n            \"stop\": False,\n            \"continuation\": None,\n        }\n        # handle observation\n        if obs is not None:\n            msg[\"value\"] = obs\n            msg[\"is_observed\"] = True\n        # apply the stack and return its return value\n        apply_stack(msg)\n        return msg[\"value\"]\n\n\ndef observation_sample(name, fn, *args, **kwargs):\n    return sample_with_type(\"observation_sample\", name, fn, *args, **kwargs)\n\n\ndef compute_design(name, fn, *args, **kwargs):\n    return sample_with_type(\"design_sample\", name, fn, *args, **kwargs)\n\n\ndef latent_sample(name, fn, *args, **kwargs):\n    return sample_with_type(\"latent_sample\", name, fn, *args, **kwargs)\n\nimport os\nimport pickle\nimport argparse\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\n\nimport torch\nfrom torch import nn\nfrom PriorContrastiveEstimationDiscreteObsTotalEnum,\n)\n\nfrom extra_distributions.truncated_normal import LowerTruncatedNormal\n\n\nclass EncoderNetwork(nn.Module):\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        design_dim,\n        osbervation_dim,\n        hidden_dim,\n        encoding_dim,\n        n_hidden_layers=2,\n        activation=nn.Softplus,\n    ):\n        super().__init__()\n     self.middle = nn.Sequential(\n                *[\n                    nn.Sequential(nn.Linear(hidden_dim, hidden_dim), activation())\n                    for _ in range(n_hidden_layers - 1)\n                ]\n            )\n        else:\n            self.middle = nn.Identity()\n        self.output_layer = nn.Linear(hidden_dim, encoding_dim)\n\n    def forward(self, xi, y, **kwargs):\n  ):\n        super().__init__()\n        self.activation_layer = activation()\n        self.input_layer = nn.Linear(input_dim, hidden_dim)\n        if n_hidden_layers > 1:\n            self.middle = nn.Sequential(\n                *[\n                    nn.Sequential(nn.Linear(hidden_dim, hidden_dim), activation())\n                    for _ in range(n_hidden_layers - 1)\n                ]\n            )\n        else:\n            self.middle = nn.Identity()\n        self.output_layer = nn.Linear(hidden_dim, output_dim)\n\n    def forward(self, r):\n        x = self.input_layer(r)\n        x experiment.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        design_net,\n        theta_loc=None,  # prior on theta\n        theta_scale=None,  # prior on theta\n        theta_dist=\"truncated normal\",\n        N=50,\n        T=2,\n    ):\n        super().__init__()\n        # theta prior hyperparams\n        self.design_net = design_net\n        self.theta_loc = theta_loc if theta_loc is not None else torch.tensor(1.0)\n        self.theta_scale = theta_scale if theta_scale is not None else torch.tensor(1.0)\n        if theta_dist == \"truncated normal\":\n            self.theta_prior_dist = LowerTruncatedNormal(\n                self.theta_loc, self.theta_scale, 0.0\n            )\n        elif theta_dist == \"lognormal\":\n            self.theta_prior_dist = dist.LogNormal(self.theta_loc, self.theta_scale)\n        else:\n            raise ValueError(\"Invalid option: `theta_dist`=%s.\" % theta_dist)\n        self.T = T  # number of experiments\n        self.N = N  # number of people\n        self.softplus = nn.Softplus()\n\n    def model(self):\n        if hasattr(self.design_net, \"parameters\"):\n            pyro.module(\"design_net\", self.design_net)\n\n        ########################################################################\n        # Sample latent variables\n        ########################################################################\n        # Theta has empty shape\n        theta = pyro.sample(\"theta\", self.theta_prior_dist)\n        theta = theta.clamp(min=1e-10, max=1e10)\n\n        y_outcomes = []\n        xi_designs = []\n\n        for t in range(self.T):\n            ####################################################################\n            # Get a design xi\n            ####################################################################\n            xi = compute_design(\n                f\"xi{t + 1}\", self.design_net.lazy(*zip(xi_designs, y_outcomes))\n            )\n            # Have to convert to positive time, and to squeeze out dimension\n            xi = self.softplus(xi.squeeze(-1))\n\n            ####################################################################\n            # Sample y\n            ####################################################################\n            death_probability = 1 - (-xi * theta).exp()\n            y = observation_sample(\n                f\"y{t + 1}\", dist.Binomial(total_count=self.N, probs=death_probability)\n            )\n            y_outcomes.append(y)\n            xi_designs.append(xi)\n\n        return y_outcomes\n\n    def eval(self, n_trace=2, theta=None):\n        self.design_net.eval()\n        if theta is not None:\n            model = pyro.condition(self.model, data={\"theta\": theta})\n        else:\n            model = self.model\n        output = []\n        with torch.no_grad():\n            for i in range(n_trace):\n\n                # print(\"Example run {}\".format(i))\n                trace = = []\n                run_ys = []\n\n                for t in range(self.T):\n                    xi = trace.nodes[f\"xi{t + 1}\"][\"value\"].item()\n                    run_xis.append(xi)\n                    # print(f\"xi{t + 1}: {xi}\")\n\n                    y = trace.nodes[f\"y{t + 1}\"][\"value\"].item()\n                    run_ys.append(y)\n                    # print(f\"y{t + 1}: {y}\")\n\n                run_df = pd.DataFrame(\n                    {\n                        \"designs\": run_xis,\n                        \"observations\": run_ys,\n                        \"order\": list(range(1, self.T + 1)),\n                    }\n                )\n                run_df[\"run_id\"] = i + 1\n                run_df[\"theta\"] = true_theta\n                output.append(run_df)\n                # print(\"-------- * --------\")\n\n        print(\"returning output\")\n        return pd.concat(output)\n\n    def rollout(self, n_rollout, grid):\n        self.design_net.eval()\n\n        grid_size = grid.shape[0]\n\n        def vectorized_model():\n            with pyro.plate(\"vectorization\", n_rollout):\n                return self.model()\n\n        with torch.no_grad():\n            trace = pyro.poutine.trace(vectorized_model).get_trace()\n            trace.nodes[\"theta\"][\"value\"] = torch.tensor([1.50], device=trace.nodes[\"theta\"][\"value\"].device)\n            trace = prune_subsample_sites(trace)\n            trace.compute_log_prob()\n\n            data = {\n                name: lexpand(node[\"value\"], grid_size)\n                for name, node in trace.nodes.items()\n                if node.get(\"subtype\") in [\"observation_sample\", \"design_sample\"]\n            }\n            data[\"theta\"] = rexpand(grid, n_rollout)\n\n            def conditional_model():\n                with pyro.plate_stack(\"vectorization\", (grid_size, n_rollout)):\n                    pyro.condition(self.model, data=data)()\n\n            condition_trace = pyro.poutine.trace(conditional_model).get_trace()\n            condition_trace = prune_subsample_sites(condition_trace)\n            condition_trace.compute_log_prob()\n\n        return condition_trace\n\n\ndef single_run(\n    seed,\n    num_steps,\n    num_inner_samples,  # L in denom\n    num_outer_samples,  # N to estimate outer E\n    lr,  # learning rate of adam optim\n    gamma,  # scheduler for adam optim\n    T,  # number of experiments\n    N,  # number of people\n    device,\n    hidden_dim,\n    encoding_dim,\n    num_layers,\n    arch,\n    complete_enum,\n    mlflow_experiment_name,\n):\n\n    pyro.clear_param_store()\n    seed = auto_seed(seed)\n    pyro.set_rng_seed(seed)\n    mlflow.set_experiment(mlflow_experiment_name)\n\n    mlflow.log_param(\"seed\", seed)\n    mlflow.log_param(\"num_experiments\", T)\n    mlflow.log_param(\"lr\", lr)\n    mlflow.log_param(\"hidden_dim\", hidden_dim)\n    mlflow.log_param(\"encoding_dim\", encoding_dim)\n    mlflow.log_param(\"num_layers\", num_layers)\n    mlflow.log_param(\"gamma\", gamma)\n    mlflow.log_param(\"complete_enum\", gamma)\n    mlflow.log_param(\"arch\", arch)\n    mlflow.log_param(\"num_steps\", num_steps)\n    mlflow.log_param(\"num_inner_samples\", num_inner_samples)\n    mlflow.log_param(\"num_outer_samples\", num_outer_samples)\n    ## set up model\n    if arch == \"static\":\n        design_net = BatchDesignBaseline(T, 1).to(device)\n    else:\n        encoder = EncoderNetwork(\n            1, 1, hidden_dim, encoding_dim, n_hidden_layers=num_layers\n        )\n        emitter = EmitterNetwork(\n            encoding_dim, hidden_dim, 1, ).to(device)\n        else:\n            raise ValueError(f\"Unexpected architecture specification: '{arch}'.\")\n\n    theta_prior_loc = torch.tensor(1.0, device=device)\n    theta_prior_scale = torch.tensor(1.0, device=device)\n    death_process = DeathProcess(\n        design_net=design_net,\n        theta_loc=theta_prior_loc,\n        theta_scale=theta_prior_scale,\n        T=T,\n        N=N,\n    )\n\n    # Annealed LR optimiser --------\n    optimizer = torch.optim.Adam\n    scheduler = pyro.optim.ExponentialLR(\n        {\n            \"optimizer\": optimizer,\n            \"optim_args\": {\"lr\": lr, \"betas\": [0.9, 0.999], \"weight_decay\": 0,},\n            \"gamma\": gamma,\n        }\n    )\n    if complete_enum:\n        pce_loss = PriorContrastiveEstimationDiscreteObsTotalEnum(\n            num_outer_samples, num_inner_samples\n        )\n    else:\n        pce_loss = PriorContrastiveEstimationScoreGradient(\n            num_outer_samples, num_inner_samples\n        )\n    oed = OED(death_process.model, scheduler, pce_loss)\n    # ----------\n\n    # optimise\n    loss_history = []\n    t = trange(1, num_steps + 1, desc=\"Loss: 0.000 \")\n    for i in t:\n        loss = oed.step()\n        loss = torch_item(loss)\n        t.set_description(\"Loss: {:.3f} \".format(loss))\n        loss_history.append(loss)\n        if i % 50 == 0:\n            mlflow.log_metric(\"loss\", oed.evaluate_loss())\n        if i % 1000 == 0:\n            scheduler.step()\n\n    mlflow.log_metric(\n        \"loss_diff50\", np.mean(loss_history[-51:-1]) / np.mean(loss_history[0:50]) - 1\n    )\n    # evaluate and store results\n    runs_output = death_process.eval()\n    results = {\n        \"design_network\": design_net.cpu(),\n        \"seed\": seed,\n        \"loss_history\": loss_history,\n        \"runs_output\": runs_output,\n    }\n\n    # Log model\n    print(\"Storing model to MlFlow... \", end=\"\")\n    # store the model:\n    mlflow.pytorch.log_model(death_process.cpu(), \"model\")\n    ml_info = mlflow.active_run().info\n    model_loc = f\"mlruns/{ml_info.experiment_id}/{ml_info.run_id}/artifacts/model\"\n    print(f\"Model sotred in {model_loc}.\")\n\n    print(f\"Run completed {mlflow.active_run().info.artifact_uri}.\")\n    print(f\"The experiment-id of this run is {ml_info.experiment_id}\")\n\n    # --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    return results",
    "Experiment Result": "The DAD method is implemented and evaluated on a Death Process model. The experimental settings for a single run are configured via command-line arguments, with the following default values:\n- `seed`: -1 (for automatic seeding).\n- `num_steps`: 5000 (number of optimization steps).\n- `num_inner_samples`: 100 (L in the denominator of the PCE loss, representing inner samples).\n- `num_outer_samples`: 200 (N to estimate the outer expectation, representing outer samples).\n- `lr`: 0.001 (learning rate for the Adam optimizer).\n- `gamma`: 0.95 (decay rate for the ExponentialLR scheduler).\n- `num_experiments` (T): 4 (number of experimental steps/stages).\n- `num_people` (N): 50 (number of subjects in the Death Process model).\n- `device`: 'cuda' (computational device, e.g., GPU).\n- `hidden_dim`: 128 (dimension of hidden layers in encoder/emitter networks).\n- `encoding_dim`: 16 (dimension of the pooled history representation).\n- `num_layers`: 2 (number of hidden layers in encoder/emitter networks).\n- `arch`: 'sum' (architecture for the design network, 'sum' corresponds to `SetEquivariantDesignNetwork`).\n- `complete_enum`: False (boolean flag to use `PriorContrastiveEstimationDiscreteObsTotalEnum` if true, otherwise `PriorContrastiveEstimationScoreGradient`).\n- `mlflow_experiment_name`: 'Default' (name for MLflow experiment logging)."
}{
    "Title": "Scalable Bayesian Optimization via Focalized Sparse Gaussian Processes",
    "Main Contributions": "This paper addresses the limitation of Bayesian optimization (BO) in high-dimensional and large-budget problems due to the cubic complexity of Gaussian Process (GP) surrogates. Existing sparse GP models often lead to overly-smooth estimations and are primarily evaluated on low-dimensional problems with large online samples. The authors propose FocalBO, which employs a focalized GP model with a novel variational loss function for stronger local prediction, and a hierarchical acquisition optimization strategy. FocalBO efficiently allocates representational power to relevant regions, leveraging large offline and online datasets to achieve state-of-the-art performance in high-dimensional tasks like robot morphology design and controlling a 585-dimensional musculoskeletal system.",
    "Methodology": "The core methodology involves two main components: 1) **Focalized GP**: A sparse GP model trained using a novel variational loss function (focalized ELBO, L2). This loss weights the data likelihood term using the maximum kernel covariance of training points to positions within a defined search region, emphasizing local functional landscapes. It also includes a regularization term to encourage accurate local prediction over blurred global estimation. 2) **FocalBO**: A hierarchical acquisition optimization algorithm that iteratively optimizes the acquisition function over progressively smaller search regions. At each iteration, it trains a specific focalized GP for the current search region, halves the search space length, and re-centers it at the current best position. A batch of inputs is proposed, and the final decision is sampled from all candidates via a Softmax distribution over acquisition values. FocalBO also adaptively adjusts the optimization depth (H) based on whether the best-performing proposed point came from a shallower or deeper search, balancing exploration and exploitation.",
    "Experimental Setup": "FocalBO was evaluated against baselines including SVGP, WOGP, and Vecchia GP, with comparisons also made to TuRBO. Experiments were conducted over 10 independent trials, reporting mean ± 1 standard error. For sparse GP models, 50 inducing variables were used for synthetic functions and 200 for other tasks. GP training used a Matern 5/2 kernel with automatic relevance determination, optimized for 1000 epochs via Adam with a learning rate of 0.01. Inducing points were initialized using Sobol sampler. Experimental tasks included: 1) **Synthetic Functions**: Shekel, Michalewicz, and 5D GP-sampled functions (Matern 5/2, lengthscale 0.5), using TS, EI, and PI acquisition functions. The setup involved 2000 random offline data points and 500 online samples (batch size 10). 2) **Robot Morphology Design**: Optimizing D'Kitty robot from Design-Bench, utilizing a 10,000-point offline dataset and 128 online evaluations (batch size 4) with EI. 3) **Human Musculoskeletal System Control**: A 585-dimensional policy for a human arm system, with 2000 random offline points and 3000 online samples (batch size 100) using Thompson sampling. All experiments were performed on a server with Intel Xeon Gold 6348 CPU, NVIDIA-A100, and 512GB memory.",
    "Limitations": "The theoretical analysis for FocalBO is not rigorous; specifically, the focalized GP cannot guarantee a constant bound for approximation error noise, though empirical results demonstrate regret reduction. The optimal optimization depth (H) for FocalBO is problem-dependent, requiring an adaptive adjustment mechanism. The paper also implicitly notes that some baselines like WOGP have extremely low computational speed, making them impractical for large datasets. While the paper highlights the benefits of FocalBO for high-dimensional and large data settings, its performance relative to simpler methods in very low-dimensional or extremely small data scenarios is not explicitly discussed as a limitation.",
    "Future Research Directions": "Future work includes theoretically analyzing FocalBO to provide more rigorous guarantees regarding its performance. Additionally, the method can be applied to more complex problems, such as large-scale parameter tuning and whole-body human musculoskeletal system control, which represent challenging high-dimensional optimization tasks.",
    "Experiment Code": "class SVGP(ApproximateGP):\n    def __init__(self, inducing_points):\n        variational_distribution = CholeskyVariationalDistribution(inducing_points.size(0))\n        variational_strategy = VariationalStrategy(self, inducing_points, variational_distribution, learn_inducing_locations=True)\n        super(SVGP, self).__init__(variational_strategy)\n        self.mean_module = gpytorch.means.ConstantMean()\n        self.covar_module = ScaleKernel(\n                    MaternKernel(nu=2.5, ard_num_dims=inducing_points.shape[1],\n                        lengthscale_constraint=Interval(0.005, inducing_points.shape[1]))\n                    )\n        self.dim = inducing_points.shape[-1]\n        \n    def forward(self, x):\n        mean_x = self.mean_module(x)\n        covar_x = self.covar_module(x)\n        return gpytorch.distributions.MultivariateNormal(mean_x, covar_x)\n    \ndef train_fvgp(\n            train_loader,\n            test_distribution,\n            # state=None,\n             induce_size=10,\n             inducing_points=None,\n             noise_max=None,\n             num_epochs=1000,\n             weight=True,\n             init=True,\n             reg=True,\n             deno=True,\n             return_mll=False,\n             return_loss=False,\n             dtype=torch.float, device=torch.device(\"cuda\" if torch.cuda.is_available() else \"cpu\")):\n    # Define the approximate GP model\n    train_x = train_loader.dataset.tensors[0]\n\n    if isinstance(test_distribution, torch.Tensor):\n        lb = test_distribution[0]\n        ub = test_distribution[1]\n        \n        lb_all = lb.expand(len(train_x), lb.shape[0])\n        ub_all = ub.expand(len(train_x), ub.shape[0])\n        nearest_x = train_x.clone()\n        nearest_x[train_x<lb_all] = lb_all[train_x<lb_all]\n        nearest_x[train_x>ub_all] = ub_all[train_x>ub_all]\n        \n        diff = torch.abs(train_x - not sum for futher weighting\n        return self.likelihood.expected_log_prob(target, variational_dist_f, **kwargs)\n    \n    \n    def forward(self, variational_dist_f, target, train_x, test_distribution, **kwargs):\n        r\"\"\"\n        Computes the Variational ELBO given :math:`q(\\mathbf f)` and :math:`\\mathbf y`.\n        Calling this function will call the likelihood's :meth:`~gpytorch.likelihoods.Likelihood.expected_log_prob`\n        function.\n\n        :param ~gpytorch.distributions.MultivariateNormal variational_dist_f: :math:`q(\\mathbf f)`\n            the outputs of the latent function (the :obj:`gpytorch.models.ApproximateGP`)\n        :param torch.Tensor target: :math:`\\mathbf y` The target values\n        :param kwargs: Additional arguments passed to the\n            likelihood's :meth:`~gpytorch.likelihoods.Likelihood.expected_log_prob` function.\n        :rtype: torch.Tensor\n        :return: Variational ELBO. Output shape corresponds to batch shape of the model/input data.\n        \"\"\"\n        # return super().forward(variational_dist_f, target, **kwargs)\n        # Get likelihood term and KL term\n        \n        # print(num_batch)\n        # do not divide for futher weighting\n        log_likelihood = self._log_likelihood_term(variational_dist_f, target, **kwargs)\n        likelihood = torch.exp(log_likelihood)\n        # Add any additional registered loss terms\n        added_loss = torch.zeros_like(log_likelihood)\n        had_added_losses = False\n        for added_loss_term in self.model.added_loss_terms():\n            added_loss.add_(added_loss_term.loss())\n            had_added_losses = True\n\n        #
